Table 2
Investments, Deposits and Treasury Income Controls
1. Q: What is the purpose and scope of the Investments, Deposits and Treasury Income procedure?
A: Show answer
The procedure establishes a controlled lifecycle for surplus-fund placements, fixed deposits, treasury approvals, counterparty eligibility, liquidity management, custody, interest income, accounting classification, valuation, maturity monitoring, reconciliation, and financial reporting. Its objective is to protect organizational funds while ensuring sufficient liquidity for program activities and maintaining reliable accounting records. The supplied specification identifies this procedure as SOP 035 and explicitly marks it as a controlled draft that has not been adopted. It also identifies important unresolved matters, including the actual legal entity, governing authority, accounting framework, tax status, donor restrictions, approval thresholds, and retention requirements. Therefore, the document should be treated as a design specification rather than authorization to place funds. Before implementation, Finance, Treasury, the independent reviewer, and the authorized governing body must validate the relevant requirements and formally approve the final procedure.

Study instruction:
- Review the scope, document status, unresolved assumptions, and approval requirements in the supplied specification.

Key point:
- A controlled draft does not establish authority to invest.
- Entity-specific decisions must be verified before implementation.

Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 11 — Investment Management
2. Q: What principles should govern the management of organizational investments and deposits?
A: Show answer
Treasury decisions should balance preservation of principal, liquidity, counterparty risk, compliance with restrictions, and investment return. The organization should not maximize interest income at the expense of its ability to meet payroll, supplier payments, emergency program expenditure, or other committed obligations. Every proposed placement must be supported by a forecast of available cash, evidence that the funds are eligible for investment, an authorized product and counterparty, approved limits, and documented approval. The UN IPSAS Finance Manual describes capital preservation and adequate liquidity as central objectives in its own investment-management environment, with risk-adjusted returns prioritized over yield alone. That framework is a useful reference, but the actual organization must apply its own authorized mandate and applicable accounting requirements. Treasury should document why the selected placement is appropriate compared with keeping funds in an operating account or using a shorter maturity.

Study instruction:
- Compare two hypothetical deposits with different interest rates, maturity dates, liquidity conditions, and counterparty risks.

Key point:
- Safety and liquidity must be assessed before expected return.
- The highest quoted interest rate is not automatically the best placement.

Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 11 — Investment Management
3. Q: How should the organization's authority to invest be established and documented?
A: Show answer
The organization must identify the governing instrument, legal form, governing-body resolution or equivalent authority, approved treasury mandate, and delegation matrix that authorize investment activities. These documents should define who may propose, review, approve, execute, settle, and monitor placements; which instruments and currencies are permitted; the maximum investment amounts and tenors; and any restrictions applicable to specific funding sources. Approval thresholds must be expressed in approved amounts, currencies, exposure calculations, and relevant aggregation rules rather than informal descriptions such as "small deposits." The mandate must also specify whether delegated authority remains valid during personnel changes or requires renewed approval. Until a valid mandate is evidenced, the proposed procedure must not authorize an actual placement or rollover. Changes to the mandate should be approved, version-controlled, communicated to affected personnel, and recorded with their effective dates.

Study instruction:
- Prepare an authority checklist identifying the governing approval, delegation, permitted instruments, limits, signatories, and effective dates.

Key point:
- Investment authority must be evidenced before execution.
- Unapproved limits or delegations must remain unresolved rather than being assumed.

Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 11 — Investment Management
4. Q: How should permitted investment instruments be selected and controlled?
A: Show answer
The treasury mandate should list approved instruments and the conditions for using each one. Depending on the organization's circumstances and authority, the policy may address fixed-term bank deposits, savings accounts, government securities, money-market instruments, pooled investments, or other financial assets. These products are not automatically interchangeable: they differ in contractual cash flows, maturity, liquidity, credit exposure, price volatility, fees, and possible restrictions on early withdrawal. For each permitted product, document eligibility criteria, authorized currencies, maximum tenor, settlement arrangements, valuation requirements, and the evidence needed before approval. Products involving leverage, derivatives, speculation, or other complex exposures should remain prohibited unless the organization has separately established the legal authority, expertise, risk controls, and explicit approval required to use them. A bank's offer of a product does not itself establish organizational permission to acquire it.

Study instruction:
- Complete an instrument eligibility matrix showing permitted products, prohibitions, approval conditions, risk characteristics, and required evidence.

Key point:
- Instrument eligibility is determined by authority, restrictions, and risk—not by availability alone.
- Different financial products require different control and accounting assessments.

Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 11 — Investment Management
- IPSASB, IPSAS 41 — Financial Instruments
5. Q: How should a bank or other financial counterparty be assessed before the organization places funds?
A: Show answer
Due diligence should establish the counterparty's exact legal identity, current authorization, applicable regulatory status, product eligibility, financial condition, operational reliability, settlement capability, and relevant contractual terms. For Indonesian institutions, the organization can use official OJK directories as an initial verification source, then retain evidence for the actual legal entity and proposed product. Review the counterparty's name, license or registration information, authorized contacts, settlement accounts, restrictions, and any applicable internal risk limits. A match in a public directory is not proof that every product offered by that institution is approved or suitable. The review should record the evidence source, date checked, reviewer, unresolved questions, and next review date. Due diligence should be refreshed periodically and before a placement when material circumstances, regulatory status, product conditions, or transaction details have changed.

Study instruction:
- Build a counterparty file using official verification evidence, contract details, risk assessment, reviewer approval, and review dates.

Key point:
- Verify the actual counterparty and product separately.
- A directory entry is evidence of identity or status, not investment approval.

Source links:
- OJK Financial Institutions Directory (FIND)
- OJK, List of Commercial Bank and Sharia Bank Head Offices
6. Q: How should deposit-guarantee protection be assessed before an investment decision?
A: Show answer
Treasury must not assume that a deposit is guaranteed simply because the counterparty is a bank or because the bank advertises deposit protection. The assessment should verify the applicable guarantee scheme, the eligible institution, product and currency, depositor identity, relevant ownership and aggregation rules, applicable limits, exclusions, and any interest-rate conditions. It must also consider the actual status of the organization's legal entity and the particular account or placement. Retain current official evidence supporting the conclusion and the date on which the assessment was made. If coverage cannot be established, record the protection status as unverified and assess the placement using the organization's approved credit-risk and counterparty limits without representing it as guaranteed. Deposit insurance, where applicable, does not replace due diligence, liquidity assessment, or authorization to invest.

Study instruction:
- Review the current official guarantee requirements and list the evidence needed to support coverage for one proposed deposit.

Key point:
- Guarantee eligibility must be demonstrated for the actual placement.
- Regulatory protection does not remove ordinary treasury controls.

Source links:
- Lembaga Penjamin Simpanan (LPS), Official Website
- OJK Financial Institutions Directory (FIND)
7. Q: How should donor restrictions and ownership of interest income be assessed before placing restricted funds?
A: Show answer
Funds received from donors, grant agreements, trusts, or other restricted arrangements may not be available for unrestricted investment merely because the cash is temporarily unused. Finance must review the executed agreement and relevant amendments for clauses governing permitted investments, approved bank accounts, currencies, custody, capital preservation, investment periods, treatment of interest, and reporting obligations. Some agreements may prohibit investment, require prior donor approval, or specify how earnings must be used or returned. The organization should identify the beneficial or contractual interest in the funds, determine whether different awards may be pooled, and document any required segregation. Interest earned on restricted funds should be separately traceable to the source and the applicable terms. If restrictions or ownership of earnings are unclear, the affected funds should remain on hold for investment decisions until the appropriate authorized decision is documented.

Study instruction:
- Review a sample grant agreement and extract its investment, interest-income, custody, and reporting conditions.

Key point:
- Available cash is not automatically available for investment or unrestricted use.
- Donor restrictions must be checked before committing funds.

Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 11 — Investment Management
8. Q: How should Treasury determine whether surplus cash is genuinely available for investment?
A: Show answer
Treasury should prepare a dated cash-flow forecast that includes opening bank balances, expected receipts, payroll, supplier commitments, program expenditure, partner transfers, taxes, debt or contractual payments, and other expected outflows. Finance must reconcile the forecast to actual bank balances and identify amounts that are restricted, pledged, earmarked, unavailable, or required for near-term operations. The organization should apply an approved liquidity buffer and stress-test the forecast for delayed donor receipts, accelerated program spending, unexpected emergencies, and late maturity proceeds. The proposed investment amount should be based on cash remaining after those needs have been assessed, rather than on the current bank balance alone. Record the forecast version, assumptions, restrictions review, reviewer, approval, and relevant dates. If a forecast is incomplete or the liquidity buffer has not been approved, the investment decision should remain on hold.

Study instruction:
- Prepare a 90-day cash forecast and identify which portion of the balance can be considered for investment after restrictions and commitments are reviewed.

Key point:
- Cash availability must be assessed against dated future obligations.
- A forecast is an essential input to investment approval.

Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 12 — Cash Management
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 11 — Investment Management
9. Q: How should investment tenor, currency, concentration, and counterparty limits be established?
A: Show answer
Limits should reflect the organization's operating needs, financial capacity, authorized treasury mandate, risk appetite, source-of-funds restrictions, and applicable legal requirements. The policy should define maximum tenor, single-counterparty exposure, aggregate related-counterparty exposure, concentration percentages, permitted currencies, and minimum liquidity coverage. Each numerical threshold needs an approved value, currency, measurement basis, effective date, and named approving authority. Related institutions should be grouped when the approved methodology requires it; otherwise, separate accounts may hide the actual concentration of risk. The limits should be tested against the post-placement exposure and the projected cash position, with existing deposits and other relevant exposures included. Until the thresholds are approved, the system should not produce a positive compliance conclusion simply because no breach has been detected. A missing limit is an unresolved control parameter, not an unlimited authorization.

Study instruction:
- Create an exposure schedule that aggregates existing positions and shows how one proposed placement would affect every relevant limit.

Key point:
- Limits require approved values and defined calculation methods.
- Missing parameters must not be interpreted as permission to proceed.

Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 11 — Investment Management
10. Q: How should duties be separated across investment request, approval, execution, settlement, and reconciliation?
A: Show answer
The process should distinguish the individual who proposes the transaction, the reviewer who assesses liquidity and compliance, the authorized approver, the person who executes the placement, the person who settles the payment, and the independent person who confirms custody and reconciles the resulting position. One person should not control the entire lifecycle or approve their own request. If staffing limitations prevent complete separation, a documented compensating control should require independent verification of the transaction, payment evidence, counterparty confirmation, and subsequent reconciliation. Delegations must be current, sufficiently specific, and checked against actual system and bank permissions. Conflicts of interest must be declared and managed by excluding conflicted individuals from affected decisions. The organization should periodically review whether the actual workflow follows the approved responsibility matrix, rather than relying exclusively on job titles or written procedures.

Study instruction:
- Map each workflow stage to its preparer, reviewer, approver, executor, and independent reconciler, then identify incompatible roles.

Key point:
- Investment initiation, approval, execution, and reconciliation require independent oversight.
- Compensating controls must be documented when full segregation is impractical.

Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 11 — Investment Management
11. Q: What steps should be completed before a placement is executed?
A: Show answer
The process begins with an investment request supported by a current liquidity forecast, verified source of funds, donor-restriction review, approved counterparty and instrument, and a limit assessment. Treasury should obtain the proposed terms, including principal, currency, value date, interest rate, day-count convention, compounding method where applicable, maturity date, fees, tax withholding, early-break conditions, and settlement instructions. An independent reviewer must compare the proposal with the authorized mandate and assess operational reasonableness. The approver must authorize the specific transaction under a valid delegation. The approved terms should then be recorded in a controlled register and transmitted to the execution and settlement personnel. The transaction must not proceed if authority, funding eligibility, liquidity, required terms, or bank instructions remain unverified. Approval must relate to the actual transaction being executed, not merely to a general proposal or earlier quotation.

Study instruction:
- Simulate a fixed-deposit request from cash forecast through terms review, approval, execution, and settlement authorization.

Key point:
- A placement requires complete evidence and approval before execution.
- The executed transaction must match the approved terms.

Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 11 — Investment Management
12. Q: How should settlement instructions and changes to bank details be authenticated?
A: Show answer
Settlement instructions must be checked against previously verified counterparty information and the authorized terms of the transaction. A change to an account number, beneficiary, bank routing detail, or settlement channel should trigger independent verification through a trusted contact method that was already on file, rather than a telephone number or email contained only in the change request. The person receiving the instructions should document the verification, and an authorized reviewer should confirm that payment details match the approved transaction. Shared credentials, informal approvals, and unverified changes must not be accepted to meet a deadline. If a discrepancy or suspicious instruction is detected, the payment should be suspended, relevant evidence preserved, and the incident escalated through the approved security and finance procedures. Any subsequent release should occur only after the discrepancy has been resolved and the required approval is recorded.

Study instruction:
- Test a scenario involving a late bank-detail change and identify the independent verification and escalation steps required before payment.

Key point:
- Bank-detail changes require verification through a previously validated channel.
- Suspicious settlement instructions must be held and investigated.

Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 11 — Investment Management
13. Q: What evidence is required to confirm that an investment or fixed deposit actually exists?
A: Show answer
The organization should retain the approved placement request, executed contractual terms, settlement or payment evidence, counterparty confirmation, ownership or custody information where applicable, and the resulting position-register record. The evidence should identify the legal counterparty, principal, currency, transaction date, value date, interest terms, maturity, account or custody reference, and settlement instructions. Independent confirmation should be obtained through an authenticated channel and compared with the organization's recorded details. A payment instruction or bank debit alone does not demonstrate that the intended deposit was opened on the approved terms. Until the required confirmation and custody evidence are received and checked, the position should remain pending or disputed according to the status definitions in the procedure. Missing evidence must be assigned to an owner for resolution and must not be treated as resolved solely because the bank statement shows an outflow.

Study instruction:
- Trace one placement from approval to bank debit, executed terms, independent confirmation, custody reference, and final register status.

Key point:
- Payment evidence and evidence of an established investment serve different purposes.
- Confirmed-active status requires independent evidence.

Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 11 — Investment Management
14. Q: What information should the investment and custody register contain?
A: Show answer
The register should identify each position uniquely and record the owning legal entity, counterparty, instrument type, source of funds, donor restriction reference, approval reference, principal, currency, placement and value dates, contractual interest rate, day-count basis, maturity date, rollover instructions, early-break conditions, account or custody reference, and confirmation status. It should also contain relevant accounting classification, measurement basis, gross accrued interest, withholding tax, fees, foreign-exchange information, valuation evidence, and general-ledger references where applicable. Status fields should distinguish proposed, approved, executed, settlement pending, confirmed active, maturing, redeemed, disputed, and closed positions. Changes to principal, rates, dates, ownership, restrictions, or classification must be recorded with effective dates, reasons, supporting evidence, and approvals. Mandatory fields and permitted status values should be controlled so that records cannot become active while material evidence is missing.

Study instruction:
- Design a sample position-register schema and identify which fields are mandatory before approval, activation, maturity, and closure.

Key point:
- A complete register links authorization, contractual terms, custody, accounting, and reporting.
- Status changes should be supported by evidence and approval.

Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 11 — Investment Management
15. Q: How should the accounting framework for investments and deposits be established before classification decisions are made?
A: Show answer
Finance must determine the actual reporting entity, applicable Indonesian accounting standards or other reporting framework, relevant standard edition, effective dates, reporting perimeter, accounting basis, functional currency, and any specific institutional policies. The framework must be supported by authoritative evidence and approved by the designated accounting authority. The UN IPSAS Finance Manual provides guidance for United Nations reporting entities; it is not automatically binding on an independent Indonesian foundation or another NGO. Likewise, IFRS 9 or IFRS 7 should not be treated as governing merely because they describe financial instruments. The selected framework determines the applicable recognition, classification, measurement, impairment, presentation, and disclosure requirements. Until the framework is verified, specific accounting conclusions should be marked unresolved rather than presented as established policy. Management should preserve the decision memo and the standards relied upon for each material conclusion.

Study instruction:
- Prepare a framework decision memo identifying the legal entity, reporting requirements, standards edition, approving authority, and accounting consequences.

Key point:
- Accounting treatment depends on the framework actually applicable to the entity.
- UN and IFRS guidance must not be assumed binding without verification.

Source links:
- IPSASB, IPSAS 41 — Financial Instruments
- Ikatan Akuntan Indonesia (IAI), Official Website
16. Q: How should a term deposit be distinguished from cash and cash equivalents and from other investments?
A: Show answer
Classification requires an assessment of the applicable accounting definition and the actual contractual and liquidity characteristics of the instrument. A product's name, its placement in a bank account, or management's intention to hold it for a short period is not sufficient by itself. Relevant considerations include the original maturity from acquisition, ability to convert the asset into a known amount of cash, risk of changes in value, restrictions on withdrawal, and the purpose for which the funds are held. The UN IPSAS Finance Manual's investment guidance describes term deposits with maturities of less than three months as cash equivalents within the UN's framework. That rule must not be imported automatically into another organization's accounts without checking the entity's applicable standard and facts. A deposit with a longer original maturity may require a different current or non-current investment classification even if it is expected to mature soon after the reporting date.

Study instruction:
- Compare three deposits with different original maturities, withdrawal conditions, and value risks under the selected accounting framework.

Key point:
- Cash-equivalent classification depends on criteria and facts, not just a short remaining maturity.
- Original maturity and restrictions should be documented.

Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 11 — Investment Management
- IFRS Foundation, IAS 7 — Statement of Cash Flows
17. Q: How should financial assets be recognized, measured, and valued after a placement?
A: Show answer
Initial recognition, subsequent measurement, valuation changes, impairment, transaction costs, and derecognition must follow the applicable accounting framework and the instrument's contractual characteristics. Depending on the relevant requirements, measurement may involve amortized cost, fair value, or another prescribed basis. The policy should identify the relevant decision criteria, valuation source, required inputs, review frequency, and journal treatment. Treasury and Finance must distinguish a deposit's contractual balance from any valuation adjustment required by the selected framework. Where fair value is required, retain the source, measurement date, assumptions, method, and independent review. Where an amortized-cost approach is applicable, preserve the contractual cash-flow schedule and necessary interest calculations. The procedure must also establish how transactions are derecognized on redemption or maturity and how gains, losses, fees, and adjustments are reported. No actual classification or measurement conclusion should be approved until the applicable framework and supporting facts are established.

Study instruction:
- Build a classification decision record showing the applicable standard, instrument characteristics, measurement basis, valuation evidence, and review approval.

Key point:
- Classification and measurement follow the governing framework and facts.
- Valuation adjustments require evidence and independent review.

Source links:
- IPSASB, IPSAS 41 — Financial Instruments
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 11 — Investment Management
18. Q: How should contractual interest terms be captured and independently checked?
A: Show answer
Finance must record the principal, currency, contractual rate, start date, end date, day-count convention, compounding frequency where relevant, payment frequency, business-day convention, and any conditions that change the rate or settlement amount. These terms should be supported by the executed contract or authenticated placement confirmation, rather than copied from an indicative quotation. The calculation method must reflect the contract and the applicable accounting policy. For a simple-interest illustration only, a principal of IDR 1,000,000,000 at an annual rate of 5% over 30 days using ACT/365 would produce gross interest of approximately IDR 4,109,589 before tax, fees, or other adjustments. Actual calculations may differ where the contract uses another convention, compounding, or a different period. A reviewer should independently reproduce the result and confirm that the workbook uses the correct terms and rounding rules.

Study instruction:
- Recalculate a sample deposit using its actual contractual terms and compare the independently computed result with the workpaper.

Key point:
- Contractual terms determine the calculation inputs.
- Numerical examples are illustrative and do not establish an approved rate or policy.

Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 11 — Investment Management
19. Q: When should interest income be accrued if the bank pays interest only at maturity?
A: Show answer
The recognition timing must follow the applicable accounting framework and the terms of the financial asset, rather than automatically following the date cash is received. Where the applicable framework requires interest to be recognized over time, Finance should calculate the amount attributable to the reporting period and recognize the appropriate income and related asset or carrying-value adjustment. The calculation must use the approved contractual information and account for the selected measurement method. At maturity, the organization must reconcile accumulated interest, the amount received, any tax withholding, fees, and any difference between prior accruals and final settlement. The precise journal depends on the instrument, its classification, and the accounting framework; therefore, a formula alone cannot determine the correct treatment. Supporting documentation should include the interest schedule, calculation date, source terms, reviewer, and related general-ledger references.

Study instruction:
- Prepare a month-end interest workpaper and reconcile accrued interest through the final maturity receipt.

Key point:
- Interest-recognition timing and cash-receipt timing may differ.
- Accruals must be reconciled when the interest is settled.

Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 11 — Investment Management
- IPSASB, IPSAS 41 — Financial Instruments
20. Q: How should gross interest, withholding tax, fees, penalties, and net cash proceeds be reconciled?
A: Show answer
The workpaper must distinguish the contractual gross interest calculation from tax deducted at source, bank or custody charges, early-withdrawal penalties, foreign-exchange effects, and the net cash received. Each component should have its own documented calculation or supporting record. Finance should compare gross interest with the contractual schedule and the bank's maturity or interest advice, independently validate the tax treatment and tax certificate, and reconcile the final net amount to bank records. Differences must not be hidden by recording only the amount received as income. The accounting entries for taxes and costs depend on the actual tax rules, the selected accounting framework, and the nature of the charge. Indonesian deposit-interest tax rules and possible exemptions or treaty conditions must be assessed by the responsible tax owner using current authoritative evidence; the organization must not apply a generic rate merely because it was used previously.

Study instruction:
- Reconcile a hypothetical maturity statement from gross interest to tax, fees, penalties, and net bank proceeds.

Key point:
- Gross income and net cash are different amounts when deductions apply.
- Tax treatment must be verified for the actual taxpayer and period.

Source links:
- Directorate General of Taxes, Income Tax on Deposit and Savings Interest
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 11 — Investment Management
21. Q: How can Finance prevent interest income from being recognized twice during accrual and settlement?
A: Show answer
The accounting workflow must distinguish the recognition of interest over the investment period from the later receipt of cash. If an interest receivable or other interest-related carrying-value adjustment has already been recognized, the maturity entry should settle that balance and recognize only any additional amount or adjustment required by the applicable framework. Finance should maintain an opening balance, current-period accrual, settlement, tax and fee deductions, corrections, and closing balance for each position. The general-ledger entries should be linked to the investment register and interest workpaper so the reviewer can confirm that the same economic return has not been credited to income twice. Automated journals should not be posted merely because a maturity transaction appears in the bank statement. They must first be compared with previous accruals and settlement records, with unresolved differences routed for review.

Study instruction:
- Prepare a journal example showing an interest accrual followed by cash settlement, and demonstrate how the accrued balance is cleared without duplicate income.

Key point:
- Accrued interest and interest received are stages of the same economic return when they relate to the same period and instrument.
- Settlement journals must reconcile to prior accruals.

Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 11 — Investment Management
- IPSASB, IPSAS 41 — Financial Instruments
22. Q: How should foreign-currency investments and the related exchange-rate exposure be managed?
A: Show answer
Treasury should assess the currency of each position against expected program expenditure, donor requirements, the entity's functional currency, and the approved currency mandate. It should evaluate whether the investment creates an exposure that could cause a shortfall when funds are required for payments in another currency. Finance must preserve the original currency amounts, contractual cash flows, relevant exchange-rate sources and dates, conversion method, and reporting-currency equivalents. The accounting treatment for exchange differences depends on the selected framework and the nature and classification of the instrument. Treasury must distinguish the investment's underlying interest return from currency gains or losses so that management can evaluate both effects. Foreign-exchange hedging should not be undertaken unless expressly authorized and supported by appropriate legal, contractual, risk-management, and accounting controls. Material exposure and valuation assumptions should be reviewed independently at reporting dates.

Study instruction:
- Assess one foreign-currency deposit against forecast expenditure and prepare a separate analysis of interest return and currency risk.

Key point:
- Foreign-currency placement decisions must reflect future funding needs and approved authority.
- Interest performance and exchange-rate effects should remain distinguishable.

Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 11 — Investment Management
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 16 — Financial Statement Presentation and Disclosures
23. Q: What controls should govern maturity monitoring and the notification of approaching deposit maturities?
A: Show answer
The maturity calendar should contain the position identifier, counterparty, principal, currency, maturity date, expected interest, settlement instructions, renewal restrictions, and responsible owner. The supplied specification proposes illustrative alerts 30, 14, 7, and 2 days before maturity, but those intervals are not approved organizational policy until formally adopted. Alerts should trigger a documented review of forecast liquidity, donor restrictions, counterparty status, alternative products, and the consequences of withdrawal or renewal. Treasury should record the decision, decision-maker, approval date, and required settlement actions. If a decision remains outstanding near maturity, the issue should be escalated according to approved deadlines. After the maturity event, Finance must verify the return of principal and interest, investigate any shortfall, and update the register and accounting records. A missed notification must not lead to an unauthorized automatic rollover.

Study instruction:
- Test whether a sample maturity is identified through all configured alert dates and leads to a documented, authorized decision.

Key point:
- Maturity alerts must result in an actual decision and assigned actions.
- Illustrative alert intervals must be approved before production use.

Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 11 — Investment Management
24. Q: How should deposit renewal, early withdrawal, and redemption decisions be controlled?
A: Show answer
Renewal must be treated as a new decision requiring review of liquidity, restrictions, counterparty eligibility, updated terms, approved exposure limits, and the relevant authority. The procedure should disable automatic rollover by default unless the authorized governing body or properly delegated approver has explicitly approved it for the exact position and terms. Early withdrawal requires an assessment of contractual penalties, lost interest, operational needs, and any donor or legal restrictions. Redemption instructions must use verified settlement details and be independently checked against the approved decision. The organization should record whether the transaction was redeemed at maturity, renewed, or terminated early, together with the principal, interest, penalties, fees, and resulting accounting entries. A decision to renew should not be inferred from silence, staff absence, an expiring quotation, or a bank's standard operating practice.

Study instruction:
- Compare a maturity renewal with an early-break scenario and identify the approvals, calculations, and evidence required for each.

Key point:
- Renewal is an explicitly authorized decision, not a default action.
- Early withdrawal requires assessment of contractual and financial consequences.

Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 11 — Investment Management
25. Q: How should Treasury reconcile investment positions with bank records, custody confirmations, the general ledger, and management reports?
A: Show answer
The reconciliation should compare independently obtained evidence across the bank statement, placement confirmation, position and custody register, interest workpaper, general ledger, and reporting schedules. It should cover principal, currency, opening and closing positions, interest receivable, interest received, taxes, fees, penalties, foreign-exchange movements, maturities, redemptions, and valuation adjustments where applicable. Every position should map to the appropriate reporting categories, with approved allocation rules documented if a position must be split. Record source totals, destination totals, the number of matched and unmatched records, residual differences, reviewer, and closure evidence. A discrepancy must be investigated rather than resolved through an unsupported journal or a manual change to the register. Material unexplained differences, missing custody evidence, or stale mapping decisions should prevent closure or release of the affected report until the required evidence is available.

Study instruction:
- Perform an end-to-end reconciliation for one period and identify how every balance is supported or escalated.

Key point:
- Independent reconciliations provide evidence of completeness, existence, and accuracy.
- Unexplained differences must remain visible until resolved.

Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 11 — Investment Management
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 16 — Financial Statement Presentation and Disclosures
26. Q: How should counterparty credit deterioration and possible impairment of investments be assessed?
A: Show answer
Treasury and Finance should monitor indicators such as missed payments, delayed settlement, adverse regulatory developments, material deterioration in the counterparty's financial condition, disputes, and evidence that contractual cash flows may not be received as expected. The response should follow the accounting framework applicable to the instrument. Under an applicable expected-credit-loss framework, the assessment differs from a framework that requires another impairment methodology; the organization must not substitute an outdated rule or a generic percentage for the required accounting analysis. The procedure should define how evidence is obtained, who performs the assessment, how assumptions are reviewed, and how any necessary adjustment is recorded. An operational concern may require the placement to be escalated or further placements suspended even before an accounting impairment is concluded. Keep the credit-risk assessment, accounting conclusion, approval, and corrective actions separately documented but linked to the position record.

Study instruction:
- Assess a hypothetical delayed maturity payment and distinguish the operational response from the required accounting impairment analysis.

Key point:
- Credit monitoring and impairment assessment are connected but distinct controls.
- The required impairment method depends on the applicable accounting standard.

Source links:
- IPSASB, IPSAS 41 — Financial Instruments
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 11 — Investment Management
27. Q: What valuation and financial-instrument disclosures should be considered at the reporting date?
A: Show answer
Finance should identify the information required by the organization's selected accounting framework and reporting purpose. Depending on the applicable requirements and materiality, the reporting package may need information on instrument classes, carrying amounts, measurement categories, valuation methods, fair-value hierarchy, credit risk, liquidity risk, interest-rate sensitivity, currency exposure, and relevant concentrations. Not every disclosure applies to every deposit or entity; the required set must be determined from the actual framework, instrument characteristics, and material risks. Retain valuation sources, assumptions, methods, review approvals, and reconciliation to the register and general ledger. Where cash or investments are restricted or unavailable for use by the reporting entity, consider the disclosure requirements of the applicable reporting framework. Any unresolved classification, valuation, or risk-information gaps should be assigned to an owner and assessed before the financial statements or other affected reports are released.

Study instruction:
- Prepare a reporting checklist that maps each instrument category and material risk to the specific disclosure requirement under the selected framework.

Key point:
- Disclosure requirements depend on the framework, instrument, and material risks.
- Reports must reconcile to supporting records and reviewed valuation evidence.

Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 16 — Financial Statement Presentation and Disclosures
- IPSASB, IPSAS 41 — Financial Instruments
28. Q: How should investment balances and earnings be reported to donors and project managers?
A: Show answer
The organization should map each investment and related income to the relevant fund, donor agreement, project, currency, and reporting period. The reporting treatment must reflect the actual source-of-funds restrictions, contractual requirements, and approved accounting policy. Reports should distinguish principal, gross interest, withholding tax, fees, realized or unrealized valuation movements where applicable, and net cash receipts. They should also indicate whether earnings are restricted, available for a designated project, required to be returned, or awaiting a documented interpretation of the agreement. The same income must not be counted twice in fund and project reports, and restricted resources must not be presented as freely available merely because the cash is held in a general bank account. Where multiple projects share a pooled placement, the allocation method must be approved, consistently applied, and reconciled to the total amount in the underlying records.

Study instruction:
- Trace one restricted deposit and its interest through the investment register, project ledger, donor report, and supporting agreement.

Key point:
- Donor reporting must reflect fund restrictions and authorized allocation rules.
- Principal and investment earnings should be separately traceable.

Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 11 — Investment Management
29. Q: How should suspected fraud, unauthorized investment activity, or missing principal be handled?
A: Show answer
Suspected fraud, forged confirmations, unauthorized placements or renewals, missing principal, suspicious changes to bank instructions, and compromised credentials should trigger immediate incident handling. The organization should preserve relevant emails, documents, bank statements, transaction logs, approvals, confirmation records, and system-access evidence. The designated incident owner should assess immediate financial exposure, suspend unsafe further transactions where authorized, notify the responsible management and control functions, and follow the established legal, regulatory, insurance, or law-enforcement escalation process when required. Staff should not alter or delete records to resolve inconsistencies, and an investigation should not depend solely on explanations from the person who initiated the transaction. The incident register should identify severity, affected position, detection time, evidence, containment actions, responsible owner, deadlines, root cause, corrective action, independent retesting, and authorized closure. Open incidents must remain visible in the treasury reporting process.

Study instruction:
- Simulate an unauthorized rollover and identify the required containment, evidence preservation, notifications, investigation, and closure controls.

Key point:
- Suspected fraud requires prompt escalation and preservation of evidence.
- An incident cannot be closed without corrective action and independent verification.

Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 11 — Investment Management
30. Q: How should system access, bank signatories, and conflicts of interest be controlled?
A: Show answer
Maintain a register of authorized users and signatories containing their roles, permitted actions, approval basis, effective dates, review dates, and revocation dates. Access should be limited to what is necessary for the person's assigned duties. Review privileged access, external adviser access, dormant accounts, changes in employment, and role transfers. Independently verify that actual system rights and bank mandates agree with the approved delegation matrix. Conflicts of interest should be declared and assessed; conflicted persons must be excluded from decisions where their involvement could compromise impartiality. Shared credentials or the use of another person's account must not be accepted as a workaround. Retain access-review evidence and approval records, but never include passwords, authentication codes, security tokens, or other secrets in the register or shared workbooks.

Study instruction:
- Compare current bank signatories and system permissions with the approved authority matrix and document every inconsistency.

Key point:
- Access must follow approved responsibilities and be removed when no longer needed.
- Conflict declarations and access changes require traceable review.

Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 11 — Investment Management
31. Q: What documentation and retention controls are required for treasury records?
A: Show answer
Records should make it possible to reconstruct the full lifecycle of each placement, including authority, funding restrictions, counterparty due diligence, proposal, approval, executed terms, verified settlement instructions, payment evidence, independent confirmation, custody, interest calculations, tax documentation, fees, valuations, maturity decisions, journal entries, reconciliations, and reporting outputs. The organization should use a stable file-naming convention, unique identifiers, controlled access, version history, and an approved retention schedule. The schedule must reflect applicable law, donor and contractual requirements, organizational policy, privacy obligations, and any legal hold. Backup arrangements must be tested through recovery procedures rather than assumed effective because copies exist. Evidence supporting a final report must remain retrievable even after the operational workbook has moved to another period. Sensitive banking and donor information should be stored in approved secure repositories with appropriate access restrictions.

Study instruction:
- Select one closed placement and verify that its complete approval, transaction, custody, accounting, and reporting file can be retrieved.

Key point:
- A complete audit trail connects every material decision to its evidence.
- Retention periods and access rights must be formally approved.

Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 11 — Investment Management
32. Q: How can Excel and Power Query improve the investment and deposit control process?
A: Show answer
Excel and Power Query can help consolidate bank statements, deposit confirmations, position registers, contractual interest schedules, general-ledger extracts, and maturity data into standardized tables. Power Query can normalize date and currency formats, map counterparties, identify missing records, and generate candidate matches for reconciliation. Excel tables and formulas can calculate contractual interest using approved inputs, track maturity dates, and provide exposure and exception summaries. However, source completeness and data transformations must be checked, and calculated outputs should be independently validated against contractual terms and accounting requirements. The workbook must distinguish imported source values from calculated fields and manually approved overrides. It must also preserve refresh dates, source references, transformation logic, and error records. Neither a successful refresh nor a green dashboard status proves that a placement is authorized, correctly classified, or supported by independent custody evidence.

Study instruction:
- Build a sample query that consolidates a deposit register, bank statement, and interest workpaper, then validate the resulting totals independently.

Key point:
- Automation can improve consistency and exception detection.
- Software cannot replace accounting judgment or transaction approval.

Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 11 — Investment Management
- Microsoft Learn — What Is Power Query?
- Microsoft Learn — Power Query User Interface
33. Q: What validation and testing procedures should be applied to automated interest calculations and reconciliation outputs?
A: Show answer
Before production use, the organization should test complete, incomplete, unusual, and invalid data. Test cases should include missing principal, invalid dates, incorrect maturity, unsupported currencies, duplicate transaction identifiers, changed interest rates, negative or unexpected amounts, missing withholding-tax evidence, mismatched bank receipts, and settlement differences. Interest calculations must be independently recalculated using a separate method or calculator and compared with the workbook results using the contract's day-count convention and approved rounding. Reconciliation tests should check both record counts and monetary totals before and after transformation, and every unmatched item should appear in an exception list. Negative tests must confirm that missing authority, unverified terms, and failed confirmations prevent activation or closure where required. Results should identify the test case, expected outcome, actual result, reviewer, defects, corrections, and retest evidence before release approval.

Study instruction:
- Design positive and negative tests for the interest workpaper, maturity calendar, position register, and reconciliation process.

Key point:
- Both calculation accuracy and control behavior must be tested.
- Critical failed tests must block release until corrected and independently retested.

34. Q: How should changes to interest rates, valuation methods, approval limits, or other treasury parameters be managed?
A: Show answer
Every material parameter change should have a documented source, business justification, impact assessment, effective date, author, reviewer, and approving authority. The assessment should consider existing positions, unexpired contracts, maturity alerts, cash forecasts, donor restrictions, tax calculations, accounting classification, reports, historical comparisons, and outstanding exceptions. Changes should be tested using representative cases before they are activated. The previous value and the approval history must be retained so that prior-period calculations remain reproducible. Historical records should not be silently overwritten when a rate, classification, or mapping changes. If a change affects prior periods or a previously approved report, Finance must evaluate whether correction or restatement procedures are required under the applicable rules. Only the authorized version should be released to operational users, with communication and training where necessary.

Study instruction:
- Trace a hypothetical change in deposit interest terms from its source evidence through impact assessment, testing, approval, release, and historical preservation.

Key point:
- Material parameter changes require authorization, testing, and version control.
- Historical calculations must remain reproducible.

35. Q: What should the treasury dashboard report to management each month?
A: Show answer
The dashboard should present the number and value of active positions, total principal by counterparty and currency, maturity profile, gross interest accrued and received, tax and fee deductions, liquidity-buffer compliance, limit utilization, unresolved confirmations, reconciliation differences, and overdue decisions or exceptions. Additional indicators may track the percentage of positions with current approvals and contractual terms, the number of positions missing custody evidence, counterparty reviews past due, and maturity decisions not completed by their required dates. Each metric must have a documented definition, source table, calculation, reporting date, owner, and escalation threshold. Figures should reconcile to controlled registers and general-ledger balances, while operational status must be checked against current independent evidence. Management should receive an explanation of material movements, emerging risks, and the actions required rather than only a collection of totals.

Study instruction:
- Design a dashboard with defined KPIs and reconcile every summary figure to an underlying controlled register.

Key point:
- Dashboard indicators require clear definitions and reliable source data.
- Management reporting should highlight actionable risks and overdue decisions.

- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 16 — Financial Statement Presentation and Disclosures

36. Q: How should system outages, unavailable approvers, and delayed maturity proceeds be handled?
A: Show answer
The procedure should define the responsible incident owner, authorized alternate personnel, official support channels, escalation thresholds, evidence requirements, and recovery process. A system outage or the absence of a key employee must not be treated as permission to bypass approval or bank-authentication controls. Staff should record the start time, affected position or reporting period, unavailable function, attempted recovery actions, relevant support-ticket references, and potential financial or compliance impact. Delayed maturity proceeds should be investigated against the contractual terms and settlement evidence, with appropriate contact and escalation to the counterparty. Once systems or personnel become available, the organization should complete pending activities, independently verify the result, reconcile the records, and document any corrective action. Any deadlines or alternative procedures must be governed by approved policy and the relevant external requirements.

Study instruction:
- Simulate a system outage occurring two days before maturity and document the safe continuation, escalation, and recovery steps.

Key point:
- Continuity arrangements must preserve authorization and evidence controls.
- Delayed or missing proceeds require formal investigation and follow-up.

Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 11 — Investment Management
37. Q: How should a complete fictional fixed-deposit transaction be used to test the procedure?
A: Show answer
Consider a purely illustrative deposit of IDR 1,000,000,000 placed for 30 days at a contractual annual simple-interest rate of 5%, using ACT/365. The indicative gross interest is approximately IDR 4,109,589 before withholding tax, fees, or other adjustments. The test should begin with verification of the mandate, source of funds, liquidity forecast, donor restrictions, counterparty eligibility, and approved limits. Next, it should test the specific transaction approval, authenticated settlement instructions, bank debit, executed terms, independent confirmation, position registration, and interest accrual. At maturity, the test should compare the contractual amount due with the cash received, independently verify deductions, clear any accrued interest balances correctly, reconcile the general ledger, and preserve the final evidence pack. The scenario does not establish an actual rate, tax treatment, guarantee, approval, or organizational policy; all such conditions must be verified for the real transaction.

Study instruction:
- Complete the fictional case from request to closure and record the evidence needed at each control gate.

Key point:
- An end-to-end scenario tests the relationship between treasury, accounting, and operational controls.
- Illustrative figures must never be mistaken for approved transaction terms.

Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 11 — Investment Management
38. Q: Which operational registers and control schedules should support the treasury procedure?
A: Show answer
A complete implementation should maintain controlled records for the approved mandate and parameters, counterparty and product due diligence, placement requests and immutable terms, investment positions and custody, maturity and redemption decisions, interest and tax calculations, valuation and foreign exchange, treasury-to-bank-to-ledger reconciliation, exceptions and incidents, access and conflicts of interest, accounting decisions and reporting mappings, and training and procedure adoption. Every register needs a defined owner, required fields, update frequency, review requirement, retention method, and status rules. Related records should be connected through stable identifiers so that a reviewer can trace a position from the authority that permitted it to the accounting and management-reporting outputs. The organization should avoid multiple uncontrolled lists that contain conflicting principal amounts, maturity dates, or approval statuses. Registers should be integrated where practical, while sensitive information and independent-review responsibilities remain appropriately protected.

Study instruction:
- Map each register to its purpose, owner, required fields, upstream source, downstream reports, and independent control.

Key point:
- Connected registers establish traceability across the treasury lifecycle.
- Each record needs ownership, status definitions, and independent review.

Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 11 — Investment Management
39. Q: What must happen before the treasury procedure and its supporting documents are formally adopted?
A: Show answer
Adoption should follow verification of the actual legal entity, governing mandate, delegated authority, accounting framework, tax status, counterparty criteria, approved products, liquidity parameters, donor restrictions, currencies, concentration limits, and record-retention requirements. The implementation team should test procedures, calculations, status transitions, report mappings, maturity alerts, access restrictions, exceptions, and recovery arrangements. It should also confirm that the blank procedure and worked examples remain consistent, and that fictional assumptions and demonstration values are clearly identified as such. All material open issues must have an assigned owner and must be resolved or explicitly held outside operational use. The designated independent reviewer should confirm the evidence and test results before the authorized approver signs the final version. Adoption records should capture the approved version, effective date, communication, training, implementation responsibilities, and next review date. The current controlled draft must not be represented as formally adopted without this evidence.

Study instruction:
- Build a release-acceptance checklist and identify which unresolved items block formal adoption or actual investment activity.

Key point:
- Approval requires verified authority, tested controls, and documented acceptance.
- A generated specification is not proof of operational adoption.

Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 11 — Investment Management
40. Q: How can management determine whether the treasury controls remain effective after implementation?
A: Show answer
The organization should monitor both compliance with mandatory approval gates and the operating performance of the process. Relevant indicators include active positions missing current approved terms, transactions without independent confirmation, overdue maturity decisions, counterparty evidence past its review date, limit breaches, unexplained gross-interest-to-net-receipt differences, restricted funds invested without completed agreement review, unreconciled ledger balances, and overdue corrective actions. Each indicator should specify its owner, calculation method, frequency, threshold, escalation route, and required closure evidence. Management should review trends and recurring exceptions to distinguish isolated mistakes from weaknesses in the workflow, data, systems, or training. Periodic walkthroughs should select completed transactions and verify authority, execution, custody, accounting, reconciliation, and reporting end to end. Material failures should lead to corrective action, independent retesting, and updated procedures where necessary. An indicator showing no recorded exceptions is not sufficient if the underlying data or review process has not itself been validated.

Study instruction:
- Create a monthly control-monitoring schedule and test several completed placements against both the recorded KPIs and the underlying transaction evidence.

Key point:
- Ongoing monitoring must combine measurable indicators with evidence-based testing.
- Corrective actions require independent retesting before closure.

Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 16 — Financial Statement Presentation and Disclosures
